. Indeed, for an arbitrary
t\in\Sigma
we can find a sequence
t_{1},t_{2},\ldots
converging to
t
such that
\mathrm{Rd}u_{t_{n}}=\overline{\mathrm{Mv}}_{t_{n}}\gamma
. Since
\|u_{t_{n}}\|_{\mathcal{M}}=\|u\|_{\mathcal{M}}
for all
n
, the sequence
u_{t_{n}}
admits a (non-relabelled) subsequence converging to some
v\in\mathcal{M}_{+}(\Omega)
. Weak-
\ast
continuity of
\mathrm{Rd}
implies convergence of
\mathrm{Rd}u_{t_{n}}
to
\mathrm{Rd}v
. On the other hand, it is easy to see that also
\overline{\mathrm{Mv}}_{t_{n}}\gamma
weakly-
\ast
converges to
\overline{\mathrm{Mv}}_{t}\gamma
as
n\rightarrow\infty
such that
\overline{\mathrm{Mv}}_{t}\gamma
is indeed in the range of
\mathrm{Rd}
as claimed.
Now it follows from uniform continuity of
\varphi\in C(\Theta\times\Xi)
that the mapping

\displaystyle t\mapsto\int_{\Theta\times\Xi}\varphi\>\mathrm{d}\mathrm{Rd}u_{t}

\displaystyle=\int_{\Theta\times\Xi}\varphi\>\mathrm{d}\overline{\mathrm{Mv}}_{t}\gamma=\int_{\Theta\times\Gamma}\varphi(\theta,s+tr)\gamma(\theta,s,r)

is continuous (again even uniformly) with respect to
t
.

As for uniqueness, given any other family
(\tilde{u}_{t})_{t\in\Sigma}
satisfying
u=\tilde{u}_{t}{\otimes}_{t}\mathcal{H}_{\Sigma}
and such that
t\mapsto\int_{\Theta\times\Xi}\varphi\>\mathrm{d}\mathrm{Rd}\tilde{u}_{t}
is continuous, it follows that
\mathrm{Rd}u_{t}=\mathrm{Rd}\tilde{u}_{t}
for all
t
and, from injectivity of
\mathrm{Rd}
, that
u_{t}=\tilde{u}_{t}
for all
t
as claimed.
∎

For any
u\in\mathcal{M}_{+}(\Omega\times\Sigma)
admitting a decomposition
u=u_{t}{\otimes}_{t}\mathcal{H}_{\Sigma}
with
u_{t}
uniquely defined in the above sense for every
t\in\Sigma
, we can now define an observation operator at all observation times.

Definition 83

(Dynamic observation operator)
.

If
(\Theta,\mathcal{H}_{\Theta})
is such that
\mathrm{Rd}
is injective, abbreviate

\mathcal{C}:=\{u\in\mathcal{M}_{+}(\Omega\times\Sigma)\,|\,\overline{\mathrm{Rd}}u=\overline{\mathrm{Mv}}\gamma\text{ for some }\gamma\in\mathcal{M}(\Theta\times\Gamma)\}

and denote by
u=u_{t}{\otimes}_{t}\mathcal{H}_{\Sigma}
the unique decomposition of a
u\in\mathcal{C}
from
82
.
With
(\mathrm{Ob}_{t})_{t\in{\mathcal{T}}}
the family of observation operators from
35
we define the
dynamic observation operator

\overline{\mathrm{Ob}}:\mathcal{C}\subset\mathcal{M}_{+}(\Omega\times\Sigma)\rightarrow H^{|{\mathcal{T}}|}
as

\overline{\mathrm{Ob}}u:=(\mathrm{Ob}_{t}u_{t})_{t\in{\mathcal{T}}}.

With this definition of
\overline{\mathrm{Ob}}
, the minimization problem

M

-

⁢

P
α

(
f
)

is well-defined and also admits a solution.

Proposition 84

(Existence for

M

-

⁢

P
α

(
f
)

)
.

Let
(\Theta,\mathcal{H}_{\Theta})
be such that
\mathrm{Rd}:\mathcal{M}(\Omega)\rightarrow\mathcal{M}(\Theta\times\Xi)
is injective and, if
\alpha=0
, assume that there exists
\lambda\in\mathcal{M}_{+}(\Lambda)
with
\mathrm{Ob}_{t}\mathrm{Mv}^{d}_{t}\lambda=f^{\dagger}_{t}
for all
t\in{\mathcal{T}}
. Then the minimization problem

M

-

⁢

P
α

(
f
)

is well-defined and admits a solution.

This result on existence is a direct consequence of the existence result
38
for

⁢

P
α

(
f
)

and the following equivalence of the two problems.

Proposition 85

(Equivalence of

⁢

P
α

(
f
)

and

M

-

⁢

P
α

(
f
)

)
.

If
(\Theta,\mathcal{H}_{\Theta})
is such that
\mathrm{Rd}:\mathcal{M}(\Omega)\rightarrow\mathcal{M}(\Theta\times\Xi)
is injective, the minimization problems

⁢

P
α

(
f
)

and

M

-

⁢

P
α

(
f
)

are equivalent in the following sense.

1.

If
((u_{t})_{t\in\Sigma},\gamma)
solves

⁢

P
α

(
f
)

, then
(u,\gamma)
solves

M

-

⁢

P
α

(
f
)

with
u:=u_{t}{\otimes}_{t}\mathcal{H}_{\Sigma}\in\mathcal{M}_{+}(\Omega\times\Sigma)
.

2.

If
(u,\gamma)
solves

M

-

⁢

P
α

(
f
)

, then
((u_{t})_{t\in\Sigma},\gamma)
solves

⁢

P
α

(
f
)

, where
u=u_{t}{\otimes}_{t}\mathcal{H}_{\Sigma}
is the unique decomposition of
u
from
82
.

Proof.

First we show that if
((u_{t})_{t\in\Sigma},\gamma)
is admissible for

⁢

P
α

(
f
)

, then
(u,\gamma)
with
u:=u_{t}{\otimes}_{t}\mathcal{H}_{\Sigma}
is admissible for

M

-

⁢

P
α

(
f
)

and has same cost:
Indeed, as long as
u=u_{t}{\otimes}_{t}\mathcal{H}_{\Sigma}
is actually well-defined and lies in
\mathcal{M}_{+}(\Omega\times\Sigma)
,
then the condition
\mathrm{Rd}u_{t}=\overline{\mathrm{Mv}}_{t}\gamma
for all
t\in\Sigma
automatically implies
\overline{\mathrm{Rd}}u=\overline{\mathrm{Mv}}\gamma
.
Furthermore, since
t\mapsto\int_{\Theta\times\Xi}\varphi\>\mathrm{d}\mathrm{Rd}u_{t}=\int_{\Theta\times\Xi}\varphi\>\mathrm{d}\overline{\mathrm{Mv}}_{t}\gamma=\int_{\Theta\times\Gamma}\varphi(\theta,s+tr)\gamma(\theta,s,r)

is continuous in
t
, the unique decomposition of
u
from
82
is in fact given by
u=u_{t}{\otimes}_{t}\mathcal{H}_{\Sigma}

so that
\overline{\mathrm{Ob}}u=(\mathrm{Ob}_{t}u_{t})_{t\in{\mathcal{T}}}
.
It still remains to show that
u_{t}{\otimes}_{t}\mathcal{H}_{\Sigma}\in\mathcal{M}_{+}(\Omega\times\Sigma)
is well-defined.
To this end, first note that by
17
the product measure
\overline{\mathrm{Mv}}_{t}\gamma{\otimes}_{t}\mathcal{H}_{\Sigma}=\mathrm{Rd}u_{t}{\otimes}_{t}\mathcal{H}_{\Sigma}\in\mathcal{M}_{+}(\Theta\times\Xi\times\Sigma)
is well-defined.
Next we show existence of
\tilde{u}\in\mathcal{M}_{+}(\Omega\times\Sigma)
such that
\overline{\mathrm{Rd}}\tilde{u}=\mathrm{Rd}u_{t}{\otimes}_{t}\mathcal{H}_{\Sigma}
. For this purpose we note that

\overline{\mathrm{Rd}}^{*}:C_{0}(\Theta\times\Xi\times\Sigma)\rightarrow C_{0}(\Omega\times\Sigma),\quad\overline{\mathrm{Rd}}^{*}\varphi(x,t):=\int_{\Theta}\varphi(\theta,\theta\cdot x,t)\>\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}_{\Theta}(\theta)

is well-defined and, as a direct computation shows, has
\overline{\mathrm{Rd}}
as its dual. Then, existence of
\tilde{u}\in\mathcal{M}_{+}(\Omega\times\Sigma)
such that
\overline{\mathrm{Rd}}\tilde{u}=\mathrm{Rd}u_{t}{\otimes}_{t}\mathcal{H}_{\Sigma}
follows from
89
in the appendix since

\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\varphi\in C_{0}(\Theta\times\Xi\times\Sigma)\\
\sup_{x\in\Omega,t\in\Sigma}(\overline{\mathrm{Rd}}^{*}\varphi)(x,t)\leq 1\end{subarray}}\int_{\Sigma}\int_{\Theta\times\Xi}\varphi(\theta,s,t)\>\mathrm{d}\mathrm{Rd}u_{t}(\theta,s)\>\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}_{\Sigma}(t)\\
\leq\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\varphi\in C_{0}(\Theta\times\Xi\times\Sigma)\\
\sup_{x\in\Omega,t\in\Sigma}\int_{\Theta}\varphi(\theta,\theta\cdot x,t)\>\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}_{\Theta}(\theta)\leq 1\end{subarray}}\sup_{t\in\Sigma}\int_{\Theta\times\Xi}\varphi(\theta,s,t)\>\mathrm{d}\mathrm{Rd}u_{t}(\theta,s)\\
\leq\sup_{t\in\Sigma}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\varphi\in C_{0}(\Theta\times\Xi)\\
\sup_{x\in\Omega}\int_{\Theta}\varphi(\theta,\theta\cdot x)\>\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}_{\Theta}(\theta)\leq 1\end{subarray}}\int_{\Theta\times\Xi}\varphi(\theta,s)\>\mathrm{d}\mathrm{Rd}u_{t}(\theta,s)\leq\sup_{t\in\Sigma}\|u_{t}\|_{\mathcal{M}}<\infty.

Now by
82
we can uniquely decompose
\tilde{u}=\tilde{u}_{t}{\otimes}_{t}\mathcal{H}_{\Sigma}
so that

\mathrm{Rd}u_{t}{\otimes}_{t}\mathcal{H}_{\Sigma}=\overline{\mathrm{Rd}}\tilde{u}=\mathrm{Rd}\tilde{u}_{t}{\otimes}_{t}\mathcal{H}_{\Sigma}

and thus
\mathrm{Rd}u_{t}=\mathrm{Rd}\tilde{u}_{t}
for
\mathcal{H}_{\Sigma}
-almost every
t\in\Sigma
. Injectivity of the Radon transform then implies
\tilde{u}=u_{t}{\otimes}_{t}\mathcal{H}_{\Sigma}\in\mathcal{M}_{+}(\Omega\times\Sigma)
.

Now we consider the reverse situation and show that if
(u,\gamma)
is admissible for

M

-

⁢

P
α

(
f
)

then
((u_{t})_{t\in\Sigma},\gamma)
is so for

⁢

P
α

(
f
)

with same cost (where
u=u_{t}{\otimes}_{t}\mathcal{H}_{\Sigma}
is the unique decomposition of
u
from
82
).
Indeed, by definition of the dynamic observation operator we have
\overline{\mathrm{Ob}}u=(\mathrm{Ob}_{t}u_{t})_{t\in{\mathcal{T}}}
for all
t\in{\mathcal{T}}
.
Furthermore, as in the proof of
82
it follows that
\mathrm{Rd}u_{t}=\overline{\mathrm{Mv}}_{t}\gamma
for every
t\in\Sigma
.

Since an admissible point for one problem induces an admissible point for the other problem with exactly same cost,
minimizers of one problem must induce minimizers of the other.
∎

Before considering in a second step the equivalence of

⁢

P
α

(
f
)

and

Π

-

⁢

P
α

(
f
)

, let us briefly show well-posedness of

Π

-

⁢

P
α

(
f
)

.

Proposition 86

(Existence for

Π

-

⁢

P
α

(
f
)

)
.

Let
f=(f_{t})_{t\in{\mathcal{T}}}\in H^{|{\mathcal{T}}|}
and
\alpha\in[0,\infty)
.
If
\alpha=0
, assume there exists
\lambda\in\mathcal{M}_{+}(\Lambda)
with
\mathrm{Ob}_{t}\mathrm{Mv}^{d}_{t}\lambda=f_{t}
for all
t\in{\mathcal{T}}
.
Then

Π

-

⁢

P
α

(
f
)

has a minimizer.

Proof.

We rewrite

Π

-

⁢

P
α

(
f
)

as minimization of the energy

E(u,\gamma)=\|\gamma_{\hat{\theta}}\|_{\mathcal{M}}+\frac{1}{2\alpha}\sum_{t\in{\mathcal{T}}}\|\mathrm{Ob}_{t}u_{t}-f_{t}\|_{H}^{2}+\iota_{\{\mathrm{Rd}_{\theta}u_{t}=\mathrm{Mv}_{t}\gamma_{\theta}\,\forall t,\theta\}}(u,\gamma)

over
(u,\gamma)\in\mathcal{M}_{+}(\Omega)^{\Sigma}\times\mathcal{M}_{+}(\Gamma)^{\Theta}
, where
\iota_{A}
is the indicator function of a constraint set
A
.
There exists
C<\infty
with
\inf E<C
since
E
is finite at
(u,\gamma)=(0,0)
when
\alpha>0

and at
(u,\gamma)=((\mathrm{Mv}^{d}_{t}\lambda)_{t\in\Sigma},(\mathrm{Rj}_{\theta}\lambda)_{\theta\in\Theta})
when
\alpha=0
.
We now endow
\mathcal{M}_{+}(\Omega)^{\Sigma}\times\mathcal{M}_{+}(\Gamma)^{\Theta}
with the product topology of the weak-
\ast
topologies on
\mathcal{M}_{+}(\Omega)
and
\mathcal{M}_{+}(\Gamma)
, respectively.
Then the set

B=\{(u,\gamma)\in\mathcal{M}_{+}(\Omega)^{\Sigma}\times\mathcal{M}_{+}(\Gamma)^{\Theta}\,|\,\|u_{t}\|_{\mathcal{M}},\|\gamma_{\theta}\|_{\mathcal{M}}\leq C\text{ for all }t\in\Sigma,\theta\in\Theta\}

is compact by Tychonoff’s theorem, since
\|\cdot\|_{\mathcal{M}}
-balls are compact on
\mathcal{M}_{+}(\Omega)
and
\mathcal{M}_{+}(\Gamma)
, respectively, by the Banach–Alaoglu theorem.
By
20
and
28
, any
(u,\gamma)
with finite energy satisfies

\|u_{t}\|_{\mathcal{M}}=\|\gamma_{\theta}\|_{\mathcal{M}}=\|\gamma_{\hat{\theta}}\|_{\mathcal{M}}
for all
t\in\Sigma
and
\theta\in\Theta

so that
\inf E=\inf_{B}E
.
Existence of a minimizer on the compact set
B
now follows from the lower semi-continuity of
E
with respect to the chosen topology,
which in turn follows from the weak-
\ast
-to-weak-
\ast
continuity of
\mathrm{Ob}_{t}
,
\mathrm{Mv}_{t}
, and
\mathrm{Rd}_{\theta}

as well as the weak-
\ast
lower semi-continuity of norms.
∎

Now we aim to show also equivalence of

⁢

P
α

(
f
)

and

Π

-

⁢

P
α

(
f
)

, which, similarly to the previous case, requires injectivity of the move operator
\mathrm{Mv}
.
First we obtain for
\gamma\in\mathcal{M}_{+}(\Theta\times\Xi)
with
\overline{\mathrm{Mv}}_{t}\gamma=\mathrm{Rd}u_{t}
a decomposition
\gamma=\mathcal{H}_{\Theta}{\otimes}_{\theta}\gamma_{\theta}
with
\gamma_{\theta}
being uniquely defined for every
\theta\in\Theta
in a certain sense.

Proposition 87

(Unique decomposition with move operators)
.

Assume
(\Sigma,\mathcal{H}_{\Sigma})
to be such that
\mathrm{Mv}:\mathcal{M}(\Gamma)\rightarrow\mathcal{M}(\Xi\times\Sigma)
is injective, and let
\gamma\in\mathcal{M}_{+}(\Theta\times\Gamma)
and
u\in\mathcal{M}_{+}(\Omega)^{\Sigma}
satisfy

\overline{\mathrm{Mv}}_{t}\gamma=\mathrm{Rd}u_{t}\quad\text{for every }t\in\Sigma.

Then there exists a unique family of measures
(\gamma_{\theta})_{\theta\in\Theta}
such that

\gamma=\mathcal{H}_{\Theta}{\otimes}_{\theta}\gamma_{\theta}\quad\text{ and }\quad\theta\mapsto\int_{\Xi\times\Sigma}\varphi\>\mathrm{d}\mathrm{Mv}\gamma_{\theta}\text{ is continuous for each }\varphi\in C(\Xi\times\Sigma).

Proof.

First we can argue as in the proof of
85
to obtain, from
\overline{\mathrm{Mv}}_{t}\gamma=\mathrm{Rd}u_{t}
, existence of
\tilde{u}\in\mathcal{M}(\Omega\times\Sigma)
such that

\overline{\mathrm{Rd}}\tilde{u}=\overline{\mathrm{Mv}}\gamma,

noting that injectivity of the Radon transform in
85
was only required to obtain uniqueness of
\tilde{u}
, which is not necessary here.
Now
81
implies that there is a family of measures
(\gamma_{\theta})_{\theta\in\Theta}
such that

\gamma=\mathcal{H}_{\Theta}{\otimes}_{\theta}\gamma_{\theta}\quad\text{and}\quad\mathrm{Mv}\gamma_{\theta}=\overline{\mathrm{Rd}}_{\theta}\tilde{u}\quad\text{ for $\mathcal{H}_{\Theta}$-almost every }\theta\in\Theta.

Similarly as in the proof of
82
, this implies
\gamma_{\theta}=\mathrm{Mv}^{-1}\overline{\mathrm{Rd}}_{\theta}\tilde{u}
for
\mathcal{H}_{\Theta}
-almost every
\theta\in\Theta
, and our goal is to define
\gamma_{\theta}
this way for
every

\theta\in\Theta
.
To this end, we show that
\overline{\mathrm{Rd}}_{\theta}\tilde{u}
is in the range of
\mathrm{Mv}
for every
\theta\in\Theta
: For
\theta\in\Theta
arbitrary, we can find a sequence
\theta_{1},\theta_{2},\ldots
converging to
\theta
such that
\overline{\mathrm{Rd}}_{\theta_{n}}\tilde{u}=\mathrm{Mv}\gamma_{\theta_{n}}
.
Since
\|\gamma_{\theta_{n}}\|_{\mathcal{M}}=\|\gamma\|_{\mathcal{M}}
for all
n
, the sequence
\gamma_{\theta_{n}}
admits a (non-relabelled) subsequence converging weakly-
\ast
to some
\tilde{\gamma}\in\mathcal{M}_{+}(\Gamma)
. Weak-
\ast
continuity of
\mathrm{Mv}
implies convergence of
\mathrm{Mv}\gamma_{\theta_{n}}
to
\mathrm{Mv}\tilde{\gamma}
. On the other hand, it is easy to see that also
\overline{\mathrm{Rd}}_{\theta_{n}}\tilde{u}
weakly-
\ast
converges to
\overline{\mathrm{Rd}}_{\theta}\tilde{u}
as
n\rightarrow\infty
such that
\overline{\mathrm{Rd}}_{\theta}\tilde{u}
is in the range of
\mathrm{Mv}
as claimed.
Hence we have
\gamma=\mathcal{H}_{\Theta}{\otimes}_{\theta}\gamma_{\theta}
with
\gamma_{\theta}=\mathrm{Mv}^{-1}\overline{\mathrm{Rd}}_{\theta}\tilde{u}
for every
\theta\in\Theta
.
Now it follows from uniform continuity of
\varphi\in C(\Xi\times\Sigma)
that the mapping

\displaystyle\theta\mapsto\int_{\Xi\times\Sigma}\varphi\>\mathrm{d}\mathrm{Mv}\gamma_{\theta}

\displaystyle=\int_{\Xi\times\Sigma}\varphi\>\mathrm{d}\overline{\mathrm{Rd}}_{\theta}\tilde{u}=\int_{\Omega\times\Sigma}\varphi(\theta\cdot x,t)\>\mathrm{d}\tilde{u}(x,t)

is continuous (again even uniformly) with respect to
\theta
as claimed. Given any other family
(\tilde{\gamma}_{\theta})_{\theta\in\Theta}
satisfying
\gamma=\mathcal{H}_{\Theta}(\theta)\times\tilde{\gamma}_{\theta}
and continuity of
\theta\mapsto\int_{\Xi\times\Sigma}\varphi\>\mathrm{d}\mathrm{Mv}\tilde{\gamma}_{\theta}
, it follows that
\mathrm{Mv}\gamma_{\theta}=\mathrm{Mv}\tilde{\gamma}_{\theta}
for all
\theta
and, from injectivity of
\mathrm{Mv}
, that
\gamma_{\theta}=\tilde{\gamma}_{\theta}
for all
\theta
as claimed.
∎

As before, this unique decomposition allows to show equivalence between

⁢

P
α

(
f
)

and

Π

-

⁢

P
α

(
f
)

.

Proposition 88

(Equivalence of

⁢

P
α

(
f
)

and

Π

-

⁢

P
α

(
f
)

)
.

If
(\Sigma,\mathcal{H}_{\Sigma})
is such that
\mathrm{Mv}:\mathcal{M}(\Gamma)\rightarrow\mathcal{M}(\Xi\times\Sigma)
is injective, the minimization problems

⁢

P
α

(
f
)

and

Π

-

⁢

P
α

(
f
)

are equivalent in the following sense.

1.

If
(u,\gamma)
solves

⁢

P
α

(
f
)

, then
(u,(\gamma_{\theta})_{\theta\in\Theta})
solves

Π

-

⁢

P
α

(
f
)

,
where
\gamma=\mathcal{H}_{\Theta}{\otimes}_{\theta}\gamma_{\theta}
is the unique decomposition of
\gamma
from
87
.

2.

If
(u,(\gamma_{\theta})_{\theta\in\Theta})
solves

Π

-

⁢

P
α

(
f
)

, then
(u,\gamma)
solves

⁢

P
α

(
f
)

with
\gamma:=\mathcal{H}_{\Theta}{\otimes}_{\theta}\gamma_{\theta}\in\mathcal{M}_{+}(\Theta\times\Gamma)
.

Proof.

First we show that if
(u,(\gamma_{\theta})_{\theta\in\Theta})
is admissible for

Π

-

⁢

P
α

(
f
)

, then
(u,\gamma)
with
\gamma:=\mathcal{H}_{\Theta}{\otimes}_{\theta}\gamma_{\theta}
is admissible for

⁢

P
α

(
f
)

and has same cost:
Indeed, as long as
\gamma=\mathcal{H}_{\Theta}{\otimes}_{\theta}\gamma_{\theta}
is actually well-defined and lies in
\mathcal{M}_{+}(\Theta\times\Gamma)
,
the condition
\mathrm{Rd}_{\theta}u_{t}=\mathrm{Mv}_{t}\gamma_{\theta}
for all
t\in\Sigma
and
\theta\in\Theta
automatically implies
\mathrm{Rd}u_{t}=\overline{\mathrm{Mv}}_{t}\gamma
for all
t\in\Sigma
.
Furthermore, by
20
and
28
we have
\|\gamma_{\theta}\|_{\mathcal{M}}=\|u_{t}\|_{\mathcal{M}}
for all
t\in\Sigma
and
\theta\in\Theta

and thus also
\|\gamma\|_{\mathcal{M}}=\|\mathcal{H}_{\Theta}{\otimes}_{\theta}\gamma_{\theta}\|_{\mathcal{M}}=\|\gamma_{\hat{\theta}}\|_{\mathcal{M}}
.
It still remains to show that
\mathcal{H}_{\Theta}{\otimes}_{\theta}\gamma_{\theta}\in\mathcal{M}_{+}(\Theta\times\Gamma)
is well-defined.
To this end, first note that by
17
the product measure

\mathcal{H}_{\Theta}{\otimes}_{\theta}\mathrm{Rd}_{\theta}u_{t}=\mathcal{H}_{\Theta}{\otimes}_{\theta}\mathrm{Mv}_{t}\gamma_{\theta}\in\mathcal{M}_{+}(\Theta\times\Xi)
is well-defined for each
t\in\Sigma
. Further observe that for any
\varphi\in C_{0}(\Theta\times\Xi\times\Sigma)
the mapping

t\mapsto\int_{\Theta}\int_{\Xi}\varphi(\theta,s,t)\>\mathrm{d}\mathrm{Mv}_{t}\gamma_{\theta}(s)\>\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}_{\Theta}(\theta)=\int_{\Theta}\int_{\Gamma}\varphi(\theta,z_{1}+tz_{2},t)\>\mathrm{d}\gamma_{\theta}(z_{1},z_{2})\>\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}_{\Theta}(\theta)

is continuous in
t
. Hence we can define the mapping

C_{0}(\Theta\times\Xi\times\Sigma)\ni\varphi\mapsto\left((\mathcal{H}_{\Theta}{\otimes}_{\theta}\mathrm{Mv}_{t}\gamma_{\theta}){\otimes}_{t}\mathcal{H}_{\Sigma}\right)(\varphi):=\int_{\Sigma}\int_{\Theta}\int_{\Xi}\varphi(\theta,s,t)\>\mathrm{d}\mathrm{Mv}_{t}\gamma_{\theta}(s)\>\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}_{\Theta}(\theta)\>\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}_{\Sigma}(t)

and, since
|\left((\mathcal{H}_{\Theta}{\otimes}_{\theta}\mathrm{Mv}_{t}\gamma_{\theta}){\otimes}_{t}\mathcal{H}_{\Sigma}\right)(\varphi)|\leq C\|\varphi\|_{\infty}
with
C>0
by a direct estimate, obtain that

(\mathcal{H}_{\Theta}{\otimes}_{\theta}\mathrm{Mv}_{t}\gamma_{\theta}){\otimes}_{t}\mathcal{H}_{\Sigma}\in\mathcal{M}_{+}(\Theta\times\Xi\times\Sigma)
.
We next show existence of some
\tilde{\gamma}\in\mathcal{M}_{+}(\Theta\times\Gamma)
such that
\overline{\mathrm{Mv}}\tilde{\gamma}=(\mathcal{H}_{\Theta}{\otimes}_{\theta}\mathrm{Mv}_{t}\gamma_{\theta}){\otimes}_{t}\mathcal{H}_{\Sigma}
. To this end, first note that

\overline{\mathrm{Mv}}^{*}:C_{0}(\Theta\times\Xi\times\Sigma)\rightarrow C_{0}(\Theta\times\Gamma),\quad\overline{\mathrm{Mv}}^{*}\varphi(\theta,(z_{1},z_{2})):=\int_{\Sigma}\varphi(\theta,z_{1}+tz_{2},t)\>\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}_{\Sigma}(t)

is well-defined and, as a direct computation shows, has dual
\overline{\mathrm{Mv}}
. Then, existence of
\tilde{\gamma}\in\mathcal{M}_{+}(\Theta\times\Gamma)
such that
\overline{\mathrm{Mv}}\tilde{\gamma}=(\mathcal{H}_{\Theta}{\otimes}_{\theta}\mathrm{Mv}_{t}\gamma_{\theta}){\otimes}_{t}\mathcal{H}_{\Sigma}
again follows from
89
since

\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\varphi\in C_{0}(\Theta\times\Xi\times\Sigma)\\
\sup_{\theta\in\Theta,z\in\Gamma}(\overline{\mathrm{Mv}}^{*}\varphi)(\theta,z)\leq 1\end{subarray}}\int_{\Sigma}\int_{\Theta}\int_{\Xi}\varphi(\theta,s,t)\>\mathrm{d}\mathrm{Mv}_{t}\gamma_{\theta}(s)\>\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}_{\Theta}(\theta)\>\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}_{\Sigma}(t)\\
\leq\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\varphi\in C_{0}(\Theta\times\Xi\times\Sigma)\\
\sup_{\theta\in\Theta,(z_{1},z_{2})\in\Gamma}\int_{\Sigma}\varphi(\theta,z_{1}+tz_{2},t)\>\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}_{\Sigma}(t)\leq 1\end{subarray}}\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\int_{\Sigma}\int_{\Xi}\varphi(\theta,s,t)\>\mathrm{d}\mathrm{Mv}_{t}\gamma_{\theta}(s)\>\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}_{\Sigma}(t)\\
\leq\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\varphi\in C_{0}(\Xi\times\Sigma)\\
\sup_{(z_{1},z_{2})\in\Gamma}\int_{\Sigma}\varphi(z_{1}+tz_{2},t)\>\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}_{\Sigma}(t)\leq 1\end{subarray}}\int_{\Sigma}\int_{\Xi}\varphi(s,t)\>\mathrm{d}\mathrm{Mv}_{t}\gamma_{\theta}(s)\>\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}_{\Sigma}(t)\leq\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\|\gamma_{\theta}\|_{\mathcal{M}}<\infty.

Now by
87
we can uniquely decompose
\tilde{\gamma}=\mathcal{H}_{\Theta}{\otimes}_{\theta}\tilde{\gamma}_{\theta}
so that

\mathcal{H}_{\Theta}{\otimes}_{\theta}\mathrm{Mv}\gamma_{\theta}=\overline{\mathrm{Mv}}\tilde{\gamma}=\mathcal{H}_{\Theta}{\otimes}_{\theta}\mathrm{Mv}\tilde{\gamma}_{\theta}

and thus
\mathrm{Mv}\gamma_{\theta}=\mathrm{Mv}\tilde{\gamma}_{\theta}
for
\mathcal{H}_{\Theta}
-almost every
\theta\in\Theta
.
Injectivity of the move operator then implies
\tilde{\gamma}=\mathcal{H}_{\Theta}{\otimes}_{\theta}\gamma_{\theta}\in\mathcal{M}_{+}(\Theta\times\Gamma)
.

Now we consider the reverse situation and show that if
(u,\gamma)
is admissible for

⁢

P
α

(
f
)

, then
(u,(\gamma_{\theta})_{\theta\in\Theta})
is so for

Π

-

⁢

P
α

(
f
)

with same cost (where
\gamma=\mathcal{H}_{\Theta}{\otimes}_{\theta}\gamma_{\theta}
is the unique decomposition of
\gamma
from
87
).
Indeed, as in the proof of
87
it follows that
\mathrm{Mv}_{t}\gamma_{\theta}=\mathrm{Rd}_{\theta}u_{t}
for all
\theta\in\Theta
and
t\in\Sigma
,
and
20
and
28
then imply
\|\gamma\|_{\mathcal{M}}=\|\mathcal{H}_{\Theta}{\otimes}_{\theta}\gamma_{\theta}\|_{\mathcal{M}}=\|\gamma_{\hat{\theta}}\|_{\mathcal{M}}
.

Again, since each admissible point for one problem induces an admissible point of the other with same cost, the statement on the minimizers follows.
∎

A.2
Auxiliary Results

Proposition 89

(Duality-based characterization of operator range)
.

Let
A:X\to Y
be a bounded linear operator such that
X
and
Y
admit predual spaces
X^{\#}
and
Y^{\#}
and such that
A^{\#}:Y^{\#}\rightarrow X^{\#}
is a bounded linear operator whose dual operator is
A
. Then, with
K\subset X^{\#}
a cone and
K^{\circ}\subset X
the polar cone, we have

y\in A(K^{\circ})\qquad\Leftrightarrow\qquad\sup_{y^{\#}\in Y^{\#},A^{\#}y^{\#}\in B_{1}^{X^{\#}}+K}\langle y^{\#},y\rangle<\infty\,,

where
B_{1}^{X^{\#}}
denotes the closed unit ball in
X^{\#}
.
Further, in the supremum,
Y^{\#}
may also be replaced by a dense subset of
Y^{\#}
.

Proof.

Given
y\in Y
, define

f:Y^{\#}\to\mathbb{R},\,f(y^{\#})=\langle y^{\#},-y\rangle\,,\qquad g:X^{\#}\to[0,\infty],\,g(x^{\#})=\iota_{B_{1}^{X^{\#}}+K}(x^{\#})

where
\iota_{S}
denotes the indicator function of a set
S
.0
Then
g
is continuous in
0
and
0\in A^{\#}\domain f=\range A^{\#}
.
Thus, by Fenchel–Rockafellar duality we have

\inf_{y^{\#}\in Y^{\#}}f(y^{\#})+g(A^{\#}y^{\#})=\sup_{x\in X}-f^{*}(Ax)-g^{*}(-x)\,.

Noting
g^{*}(x)=\|x\|_{X}+\iota_{K^{\circ}}(x)
and
f^{*}(\tilde{y})=\iota_{-y}(\tilde{y})=0
if
\tilde{y}=-y
and
\infty
else, the above equality turns into

\inf_{y\in Y^{\#},A^{\#}y^{\#}\in B_{1}^{X^{\#}}+K}\langle y^{\#},-y\rangle=\sup_{x\in K^{\circ},Ax=y}-\|x\|_{x}\begin{cases}=-\infty&\text{if }y\notin A(K^{\circ})\,,\\
>-\infty&\text{else.}\end{cases}

This directly implies the desired equivalence.
Since
y^{\#}\mapsto\langle y^{\#},-y\rangle
is continuous on
Y^{\#}
and
A^{\#}(\lambda y^{\#})\in\mathrm{int}(B_{1}^{X^{\#}}+K)
for any
A^{\#}y^{\#}\in B_{1}^{X^{\#}}+K
and
\lambda\in[0,1)
,
the supremum stays the same when only taken over a dense subset of
Y^{\#}
.
∎
